\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\bigg\{E|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+(2C_{g}+4C_{g}^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}|^{\beta}\bigg]+\delta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\delta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}[g^{1}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})]dt\bigg)^{\beta}

\displaystyle+E\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{y}\tilde{z}^{i}dW^{i}_{t}\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+E\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{y}\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dt)\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg\}.

Note that

\displaystyle E\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{y}\tilde{z}^{i}dW^{i}_{t}\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CE\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{y}|^{2}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{4}}\leq CE\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{4}}\bigg]

(2.33)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{2}}{2}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]+\frac{1}{2}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg],

and

\displaystyle E\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{y}\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dt)\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\leq CE\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{y}|^{2}|\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{4}}

(2.34)

\displaystyle\leq CE\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{4}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{2}}{2}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]+\frac{1}{2}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg].

Then the inequality (
2.32
) becomes

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.35)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\bigg\{E|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+\big[(2C_{g}+4C_{g}^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\delta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+C^{2}\big]E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\delta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}[g^{1}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})]dt\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg\}.

Meanwhile, we can obtain from the integral form of (
2.29
) that

\displaystyle|\tilde{y}|^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\bigg\{|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{t}^{T}[g^{1}(s,y^{1},z^{1,1},z^{2,1},\tilde{z}^{1,1},\tilde{z}^{2,1})-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})]ds\bigg|^{\beta}

(2.36)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg|\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{z}^{i}dW^{i}_{s}\bigg|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds)\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg)\bigg\}.

By B-D-G’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta}\bigg\{E|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+E\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}[g^{1}(s,y^{1},z^{1,1},z^{2,1},\tilde{z}^{1,1},\tilde{z}^{2,1})

(2.37)

\displaystyle-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})]ds\bigg|^{\beta}+E\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+E\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\bigg\{E|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}C_{g}(|\tilde{y}|+|\tilde{z}^{1}|+|\tilde{z}^{2}|+||\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{1}||+||\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{2}||)ds\bigg)^{\beta}

\displaystyle+E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g^{1}(s,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})|ds\bigg)^{\beta}

\displaystyle+E\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+E\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg\}.

Substituting (
2.35
), we can rearrange the above inequality as follows

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,g,T}E|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+C_{\beta,g,T,\delta}E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g^{1}(s,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})

(2.38)

\displaystyle-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})|ds\bigg)^{\beta}+C_{\beta,g,T,\delta}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg].

Let
1-C_{\beta,g,T,\delta}>0
and we get

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,g,T,\delta}\bigg[E|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g^{1}(s,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})

(2.39)

\displaystyle-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg].

Finally, the a priori estimate is given in the following

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\tilde{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]+E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.40)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,g,T,\delta}E\bigg[|\xi^{1}-\xi^{2}|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g^{1}(s,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})

\displaystyle-g^{2}(t,y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg].

The proof is complete.
∎

We now back to the proof of Theorem
2.3
.

Proof.

For any
(y^{\prime},z^{1,\prime},z^{2,\prime},\tilde{z}^{1,\prime},\tilde{z}^{2,\prime})\in\tilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\beta}[0,T]
, it is easy to get a unique solution
(y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})\\
\in\tilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\beta}[0,T]
to the decoupled FBSDEP as follows:

\left\{\begin{aligned} dx_{t}&=b(t,x_{t},y^{\prime}_{t},z^{1,\prime}_{t},z^{2,\prime}_{t},\tilde{z}^{1,\prime}_{(t,e)},\tilde{z}^{2,\prime}_{(t,e)})dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sigma_{i}(t,x_{t},y^{\prime}_{t},z^{1,\prime}_{t},z^{2,\prime}_{t},\tilde{z}^{1,\prime}_{(t,e)},\tilde{z}^{2,\prime}_{(t,e)})dW^{i}_{t}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(t,x_{t},y^{\prime}_{t},\tilde{z}^{1,\prime}_{(t,e)},\tilde{z}^{2,\prime}_{(t,e)},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dt),\\
-dy_{t}&=g(t,\Theta(t))dt-\sum_{i=1}^{2}z^{i}_{t}dW^{i}_{t}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i}_{(t,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dt),\quad t\in[0,T],\\
x_{0}&=x_{0},\ \ y_{T}=\phi(x_{T}),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.41)

by solving forward SDEP first in
S^{\beta}[0,T]
and then solving BSDEP in
\tilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\beta}[0,T]
, sequentially.
Define a map
\mathcal{X}_{1}
from
\tilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\beta}[0,T]
to itself. Let
(y^{\prime}_{i},z^{1,\prime}_{i},z^{2,\prime}_{i},\tilde{z}^{1,\prime}_{i},\tilde{z}^{2,\prime}_{i})\in\tilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\beta}[0,T]
and
(y_{i},z^{1}_{i},z^{2}_{i},\tilde{z}^{1}_{i},\tilde{z}^{2}_{i})=\mathcal{X}_{1}(y^{\prime}_{i},z^{1,\prime}_{i},z^{2,\prime}_{i},\tilde{z}^{1,\prime}_{i},\tilde{z}^{2,\prime}_{i}),i=1,2
and define

\displaystyle\hat{y}^{\prime}=y^{\prime}_{1}-y^{\prime}_{2},\ \hat{z}^{1,\prime}=z^{1,\prime}_{1}-z^{1,\prime}_{2},\ \hat{z}^{2,\prime}=z^{2,\prime}_{1}-z^{2,\prime}_{2},\ \hat{\tilde{z}}^{1,\prime}=\tilde{z}^{1,\prime}_{1}-\tilde{z}^{1,\prime}_{2},\ \hat{\tilde{z}}^{2,\prime}=\tilde{z}^{2,\prime}_{1}-\tilde{z}^{2,\prime}_{2},

(2.42)

\displaystyle\hat{y}=y_{1}-y_{2},\ \hat{z}^{1}=z^{1}_{1}-z^{1}_{2},\ \hat{z}^{2}=z^{2}_{1}-z^{2}_{2},\ \hat{\tilde{z}}^{1}=\tilde{z}^{1}_{1}-\tilde{z}^{1}_{2},\ \hat{\tilde{z}}^{2}=\tilde{z}^{2}_{1}-\tilde{z}^{2}_{2},\ \hat{x}=x_{1}-x_{2}.

By
(A4)
, we get

\displaystyle|\hat{x}_{t}|^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\bigg\{\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}C\big(|\hat{x}|+|\hat{y}^{\prime}|+|\hat{z}^{1,\prime}|+|\hat{z}^{2,\prime}|+||\hat{\tilde{z}}^{1,\prime}||+||\hat{\tilde{z}}^{2,\prime}||\big)ds\bigg|^{\beta}

(2.43)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\big[K(|\hat{x}|+|\hat{y}^{\prime}|)+L_{\sigma_{i}}(|\hat{z}^{1,\prime}|+|\hat{z}^{2,\prime}|+||\hat{\tilde{z}}^{1,\prime}||+||\hat{\tilde{z}}^{2,\prime}||)\big]dW^{i}_{s}\bigg|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\big[\rho(e)(|\hat{x}|+|\hat{y}^{\prime}|)+L_{f_{i}}(e)(|\hat{\tilde{z}}^{1,\prime}|+|\hat{\tilde{z}}^{2,\prime}|)\big]\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds)\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg\},

and

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{x}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta}\bigg\{(CT^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+L_{\sigma_{1}}^{\beta}+L_{\sigma_{2}}^{\beta})\sum_{i=1}^{2}E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\hat{z}^{i,\prime}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.44)

\displaystyle+\big(CT^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+L_{\sigma_{1}}+L_{\sigma_{2}}+\tilde{C}_{f_{1}}+\tilde{C}_{f_{2}}\big)\sum_{i=1}^{2}E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i,\prime}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\bigg(CT^{\beta}+K^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{x}_{t}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{y}^{\prime}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\bigg\}.

And there exists a
\hat{T}
such that
1-C_{\beta}\bigg(C\hat{T}^{\beta}+K^{\beta}\hat{T}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\hat{T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)>0
, then we have, for
T\leq\hat{T}
,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{x}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq\frac{C_{\beta}(CT^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+L_{\sigma_{1}}^{\beta}+L_{\sigma_{2}}^{\beta})}{1-C_{\beta}\bigg(CT^{\beta}+K^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)}\sum_{i=1}^{2}E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\hat{z}^{i,\prime}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.45)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{\beta}(CT^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+L_{\sigma_{1}}^{\beta}+L_{\sigma_{2}}^{\beta}+\tilde{C}_{f_{1}}+\tilde{C}_{f_{2}})}{1-C_{\beta}\bigg(CT^{\beta}+K^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)}\sum_{i=1}^{2}E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i,\prime}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{\beta}\bigg(CT^{\beta}+K^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)}{1-C_{\beta}\bigg(CT^{\beta}+K^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{y}^{\prime}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg].

Next, we can get the BSDEP of
(\hat{y},\hat{z}^{1},\hat{z}^{2},\hat{\tilde{z}}^{1},\hat{\tilde{z}}^{2})
in the following:

\left\{\begin{aligned} -d\hat{y}_{t}&=\big[g(t,\Theta_{1})-g(t,\Theta_{2})\big]dt-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\hat{z}^{i}_{t}dW^{i}_{t}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(t,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dt),\quad t\in[0,T],\\
\hat{y}_{T}&=\phi(x_{1T})-\phi(x_{2T}).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.46)

By the a priori estimate (
2.28
) in Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]+E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\hat{z}^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.47)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[E|\phi(x_{1T})-\phi(x_{2T})|^{\beta}+E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,x_{1},y_{1},z^{1}_{1},z^{2}_{1},\tilde{z}^{1}_{1},\tilde{z}^{2}_{1})-g(t,x_{2},y_{1},z^{1}_{1},z^{2}_{1},\tilde{z}^{1}_{1},\tilde{z}^{2}_{1})|dt\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[E\big[C\hat{x}_{T}\big]^{\beta}+E\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\hat{x}_{t}dt\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C\big(1+T^{\beta}\big)E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{x}|^{\beta}\bigg].

Then substituting (
2.45
) into (
2.47
), we can obtain

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{y}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]+E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\hat{z}^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.48)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\beta}\big(1+T^{\beta}\big)\Big[C(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T^{\beta})+L_{\sigma_{1}}^{\beta}+L_{\sigma_{2}}^{\beta}+\tilde{C}_{f_{1}}+\tilde{C}_{f_{2}}+K^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\Big]}{1-C_{\beta}\Big[CT^{\beta}+K^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+T\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\Big]}

\displaystyle\times E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\hat{z}^{i,\prime}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i,\prime}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\hat{y}^{\prime}_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg].

We take Lipschitz coefficients
L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},\tilde{C}_{f_{1}}
and
\tilde{C}_{f_{2}}
small enough,
C
is a constant not related to
T
but changed every step. Thus, there exists a
\tilde{T}\leq\hat{T}
small enough such that

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\beta}\big(1+\tilde{T}^{\beta}\big)\Big[C(\tilde{T}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\tilde{T}^{\beta})+L_{\sigma_{1}}^{\beta}+L_{\sigma_{2}}^{\beta}+\tilde{C}_{f_{1}}+\tilde{C}_{f_{2}}+K^{\beta}\tilde{T}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\tilde{T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\Big]}{1-C_{\beta}\Big[C\tilde{T}^{\beta}+K^{\beta}\tilde{T}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\tilde{T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\Big]}<1.

(2.49)

Then for any
0\leq T\leq\tilde{T}
,
\mathcal{X}_{1}
is a contraction on
[0,T]
, which has a unique fixed point
\mathcal{X}_{1}(y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})=(y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
. Moreover, FBSDEP (
2.10
) admits a unique solution
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
on small interval
[0,T]
. The proof is complete.
∎

The following assumption is needed.

(A6)
For any
t\in[0,T]
,
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})\in R^{6}
, the jump coefficient
f_{i}
is independent of
(z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
for
i=1,2
.

If we consider (
2.10
) in a parameterized form by initial condition
(t,\xi)\in[0,T]\times L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{t},P;\\
\mathbb{R})
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} dx^{t,\xi}_{s}&=b(s,\Theta_{s}^{t,\xi})ds+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sigma_{i}(s,\Theta_{s}^{t,\xi})dW^{i}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(s,\Theta_{s-}^{t,\xi},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds),\\
-dy^{t,\xi}_{s}&=g(s,\Theta_{s}^{t,\xi})ds-\sum_{i=1}^{2}z^{i,t,\xi}_{s}dW^{i}_{s}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i,t,\xi}_{(s,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds),\quad s\in[t,t+\delta],\\
x^{t,\xi}_{t}&=\xi,\ \ y^{t,\xi}_{t+\delta}=\phi(x^{t,\xi}_{t+\delta}),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.50)

where
\Theta_{s}^{t,\xi}=(x^{t,\xi}_{s},y^{t,\xi}_{s},z^{1,t,\xi}_{s},z^{2,t,\xi}_{s},\tilde{z}^{1,t,\xi}_{(s,e)},\tilde{z}^{2,t,\xi}_{(s,e)})
. Then, similar to
Theorem 3.4
in Li and Wei
[
16
]
, we can also obtain the following estimate with respect to the initial value in a conditional expectation form.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let assumption
(A4),(A6)
hold. Then, for every
\beta\geq 2
, there exists a sufficiently small constant
\tilde{\delta}
depending on
(K,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},\rho,L_{f_{1}},L_{f_{2}})
and constant
C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L}
depending on
(\beta,K,\rho,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},L_{f_{1}},L_{f_{2}},L)
such that for every
0\leq\delta\leq\tilde{\delta}
and
\xi\in L^{\beta}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{t},P;\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq t+\delta}|x^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq t+\delta}|y^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}|z^{i,t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.51)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,t,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}),\ P\mbox{-}a.s..

Motivated by the
Theorem 2.1
in the recent result in
[
21
]
, by the critical and thought-provoking technique in their paper, we give an extension result to the case with Poisson jumps. Then we introduce the first main theorem in our paper.

Theorem 2.4
.

Let
(A4),(A6)
hold, and assume that, for every
\xi,\tilde{\xi}\in L^{\beta}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{t},P;\mathbb{R})
, the
L^{2}
-estimations of FBSDEP (
2.50
) are given as follows:

\mathrm{(i)}

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|x^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|y^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}|z^{i,t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,t,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]

(2.52)

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}(1+|\xi|^{2}),

\mathrm{(ii)}

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|x^{t,\xi}_{s}-x^{t,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|y^{t,\xi}_{s}-y^{t,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}|z^{i,t,\xi}_{s}-z^{i,t,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}ds

(2.53)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,t,\xi}_{(s,e)}-\tilde{z}^{i,t,\tilde{\xi}}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]\leq K_{1}|\xi-\tilde{\xi}|^{2},

where constant
K_{1}
is positive and independent of
t\in[0,T]
.

Then FBSDEP (
2.50
) admits a unique
L^{\beta}(\beta>2)
-solution with
t=0
and any given terminal time
T
, i.e.,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

(2.54)

\displaystyle\leq K_{2}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}),

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}-x^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}-y^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}-z^{i,0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.55)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}-\tilde{z}^{i,0,\tilde{\xi}}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]\leq K_{2}|\xi-\tilde{\xi}|^{\beta},

Proof.

First, the given
L^{2}
-estimations
\mathrm{(i),(ii)}
come to show the existence and uniqueness of
L^{2}
-solution to FBSDE (
2.50
) on
[t,T]
for any given initial time and its corresponding initial value
(t,\xi)\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{t},P;\mathbb{R})
.

Next, we investigate the FBSDEP (
2.50
) at the initial time
t=0
,

\left\{\begin{aligned} dx^{0,\xi}_{s}&=b(s,\Theta_{s}^{0,\xi})ds+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sigma_{i}(s,\Theta_{s}^{0,\xi})dW^{i}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(s,\Theta_{s-}^{0,\xi},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds),\\
-dy^{0,\xi}_{s}&=g(s,\Theta_{s}^{0,\xi})ds-\sum_{i=1}^{2}z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}dW^{i}_{s}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds),\quad s\in[0,T],\\
x^{0,\xi}_{0}&=\xi,\ \ y^{0,\xi}_{T}=\phi(x^{0,\xi}_{T}).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.56)

Observing the estimation (
2.53
), a key derivation shows

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}-x^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}-y^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}-z^{i,0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}ds

(2.57)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}-\tilde{z}^{i,0,\tilde{\xi}}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]\leq K_{1}|x^{0,\xi}_{t}-x^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{t}|^{2},

then we get

\displaystyle|y^{0,\xi}_{t}-y^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{t}|^{2}\leq E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}-y^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]\leq K_{1}|x^{0,\xi}_{t}-x^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{t}|^{2},

(2.58)

which implies the uniformly Lipschitz condition of
y^{0,\xi}_{t}
with respect to
x^{0,\xi}_{t}
for any
t\in[0,T]
, i.e.,

\displaystyle|y^{0,\xi}_{t}-y^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{t}|\leq\sqrt{K_{1}}|x^{0,\xi}_{t}-x^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{t}|,\ t\in[0,T].

(2.59)

Then, for any given
T
, we divide the whole interval
[0,T]
into several small intervals with same interval length
\delta
, i.e.,
[0,\delta],[\delta,2\delta],\dots,[(k-1)\delta,k\delta]
, and without loss of generality, we assume
T=k\delta
and
k
is positive integer. For the first small interval
[0,\delta]
, under assumption
(A4),(A6)
and uniformly Lipschitz condition (
2.59
) with terminal time
\delta
, then, by Lemma
2.4
, there exists a constant
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq\delta}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq\delta}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{0}\bigg]

(2.60)

\displaystyle\leq C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|x^{0,\xi}_{0}|^{\beta}),

where the constant
C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}
depends on
K_{1},L
and Lipschitz constants
(K,\rho,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},\\
L_{f_{1}},L_{f_{2}})
. Note that the coefficients of FBSDEP (
2.50
) satisfy the same assumption in the interval
[0,T]
. Therefore, by the induction for the following interval
[(j-1)\delta,j\delta],1\leq j\leq k
and combining the uniformly Lipschitz condition (
2.59
) for terminal time
2\delta,3\delta,\cdots,k\delta
, respectively, we have, for
1\leq j\leq k
,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{s\in[(j-1)\delta,j\delta]}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{s\in[(j-1)\delta,j\delta]}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{(j-1)\delta}^{j\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.61)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{(j-1)\delta}^{j\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{(j-1)\delta}\bigg]\leq C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|x^{0,\xi}_{(j-1)\delta}|^{\beta}).

Now, by (
2.59
), we have obtained these
L^{\beta}
-estimates by Lemma
2.4
for small intervals
[(j-1)\delta,j\delta],1\leq j\leq k
, then we show that how to connect them together.

When considering the case of
j=1,2
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq\delta}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq\delta}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.62)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{0}\bigg]\leq C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}),

and

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{\delta\leq s\leq 2\delta}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{\delta\leq s\leq 2\delta}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{\delta}^{2\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.63)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{\delta}^{2\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{\delta}\bigg]\leq C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|x^{0,\xi}_{\delta}|^{\beta}),

we consider

\displaystyle E\big[C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|x^{0,\xi}_{\delta}|^{\beta})\big|\mathcal{F}_{0}\big]

(2.64)

\displaystyle\leq C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}\big[1+C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta})\big]

\displaystyle\leq(C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}+C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}^{2})(1+|\xi|^{\beta}),

and

\displaystyle C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta})+E\big[C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|x^{0,\xi}_{\delta}\big|^{\beta})|\mathcal{F}_{0}\big]

(2.65)

\displaystyle\leq C^{(2)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta})

where
C^{(2)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}=2C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}+C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}^{2}
.

Taking conditional expectation
E[\cdot|\mathcal{F}_{0}]
on both sides of (
2.63
), then adding it to (
2.62
), we get

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq 2\delta}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq 2\delta}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{\delta}^{2\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)

(2.66)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{\delta}^{2\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{0}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C^{(2)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}),

by utilizing the inequality

(a+b)^{k}\leq 2^{k}(a^{k}+b^{k}),\ a,b\geq 0,\ k\geq 1.

(2.67)

Then we have, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{2\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\leq\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{\delta}^{2\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}},

(2.68)

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{2\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\leq\bigg(\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{\delta}^{2\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}.

Let
\tilde{C}^{(2)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}=2^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{(2)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq 2\delta}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq 2\delta}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{2\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.69)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{2\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{0}\bigg]\leq\tilde{C}^{(2)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}).

Next, we continue to consider the case of
j=3
,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{2\delta\leq s\leq 3\delta}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{2\delta\leq s\leq 3\delta}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{2\delta}^{3\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.70)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{2\delta}^{3\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{2\delta}\bigg]\leq C_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|x^{0,\xi}_{2\delta}|^{\beta}).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq 3\delta}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq 3\delta}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{3\delta}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.71)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{3\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{0}\bigg]\leq\tilde{C}^{(3)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}).

By induction for
j\geq 4
, we obtain

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.72)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{0}\bigg]\leq\tilde{C}^{(k)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}).

Finally, by taking
K_{2}=\tilde{C}^{(k)}_{K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L,K_{1}}
, then we prove the
L^{\beta}
-estimation (
2.54
) and can also obtain (
2.55
) similarly, which guarantee the existence and uniqueness of
L^{\beta}
-solution to FBSDEP (
2.50
) with
t=0
, respectively.
∎

Now, we can give the
L^{\beta}(\beta>2)
-solution and its estimate for FBSDEP (
2.50
) in
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\beta}[0,T]
. The following lemma can be obtained similar to Lemma
2.4
by referring to the result in
[
16
]
.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let assumption
(A4),(A5)
hold. Then, for every
\beta\geq 2
, there exists a sufficiently small constant
\tilde{\delta}
depending on
(K,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},\rho,L_{f_{1}},L_{f_{2}})
and constant
C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L}
depending on
(\beta,K,\rho,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},L_{f_{1}},L_{f_{2}},L)
such that for every
0\leq\delta\leq\tilde{\delta}
and
\xi\in L^{\beta}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{t},P;\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq t+\delta}|x^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq t+\delta}|y^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}|z^{i,t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.73)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,t,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},L}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}),\ P\mbox{-}a.s..

Similarly, we can also obtain the following important theorem based on
Theorem 2.1
in
[
21
]
, which shows that, for any given terminal time
T
, under some
L^{2}
-estimate, the
L^{2}
-solution are the
L^{\beta}
-solution in
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\beta}[0,T](\beta>2)
. So we omit the detailed proof.

Theorem 2.5
.

Let
(A4),(A5)
hold, and assume that, for every
\xi,\tilde{\xi}\in L^{\beta}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{t},P;\mathbb{R})
, the
L^{2}
-estimations of FBSDEP (
2.50
) are given as follows:

\mathrm{(i)}

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|x^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|y^{t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{t}^{T}|z^{i,t,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,t,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]

(2.74)

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}(1+|\xi|^{2}),

\mathrm{(ii)}

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|x^{t,\xi}_{s}-x^{t,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|y^{t,\xi}_{s}-y^{t,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{t}^{T}|z^{i,t,\xi}_{s}-z^{i,t,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}ds

(2.75)

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,t,\xi}_{(s,e)}-\tilde{z}^{i,t,\tilde{\xi}}_{(s,e)}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]\leq K_{1}|\xi-\tilde{\xi}|^{2},

where constant
K_{1}
is positive and independent of
t\in[0,T]
.

Then FBSDEP (
2.50
) admits a unique
L^{\beta}(\beta>2)
-solution with
t=0
and any given terminal time
T
, i.e.,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.76)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\leq K_{2}(1+|\xi|^{\beta}),

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|x^{0,\xi}_{s}-x^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|y^{0,\xi}_{s}-y^{0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i,0,\xi}_{s}-z^{i,0,\tilde{\xi}}_{s}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.77)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(s,e)}-\tilde{z}^{i,0,\tilde{\xi}}_{(s,e)}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\leq K_{2}|\xi-\tilde{\xi}|^{\beta}.

Then we give the
L^{\beta}(\beta\geq 2)
-estimate for fully-coupled FBSDEP.

Theorem 2.6
.

Suppose that
(A4), (A6)
hold. Then, for any
\beta\geq 2
, suppose that
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\\
\tilde{z}^{2})
is the solution to (
2.10
), there exist a sufficiently small constant
\tilde{\tilde{T}}>0
depending on
K,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},\rho
and some constant
C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}
depending on
\beta
and Lipschitz constants
K,\rho,L_{\sigma_{1}}
,
L_{\sigma_{2}}
, such that, for every
0\leq T\leq\tilde{\tilde{T}}
,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}_{t}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.78)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}E\bigg[|x_{0}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|dt\bigg)^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|dt\bigg)^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(t,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg].

Moreover, Let
(x^{1},y^{1},z^{1,1},z^{2,1},\tilde{z}^{1,1},\tilde{z}^{2,1}),(x^{2},y^{2},z^{1,2},z^{2,2},\tilde{z}^{1,2},\tilde{z}^{2,2})
be two solutions with different initial values
x^{1}_{0},x^{2}_{0}
, respectively. Then we also give an estimate with respect to the initial value in the following

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x^{1}_{t}-x^{2}_{t}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y^{1}_{t}-y^{2}_{t}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i,1}_{t}-z^{i,2}_{t}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.79)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i,1}-\tilde{z}^{i,2}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\leq C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}|x^{1}_{0}-x^{2}_{0}|^{\beta}.

Proof.

The second estimate (
2.79
) are the direct result of (
2.78
), so we mainly prove the first estimate. We should note that, as we analyzed in Remark
2.3
, under
(A6)
, we have obtained the existence and uniqueness of the solution to the fully coupled FBSDEP (
2.10
), which can guarantee the
\beta
-integrability in the following. From the backward equation in (
2.10
),
y
is given by the right-continuous version of
y_{t}=E\big[\phi(x_{T})+\int_{t}^{T}g(t,x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})ds|\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]
. Since
\beta\geq 2
, by the martingale representation theorem in
[
41
]
for local square integrable martingale,
(z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
corresponds to the unique pair of
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
-predictable processes satisfying

\displaystyle E\Big[\phi(x_{T})+\int_{0}^{T}g(t,x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})ds\big|\mathcal{F}_{t}\Big]

\displaystyle=y_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}z^{i}_{s}dW^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i}_{(s,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds)\bigg),\ P\mbox{-}a.s.,

we have
|y_{t}|\leq E\big[|\phi(x_{T})|+\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})|ds|\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]
. Using martingale inequality, we get

\displaystyle E\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}\Big]\leq E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigg|E\Big[|\phi(x_{T})|+\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,\Theta)|ds\Big|\mathcal{F}_{t}\Big]\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg]

(2.80)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\Big|E\Big[|\phi(x_{T})|+\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,\Theta)|ds\Big|\mathcal{F}_{T}\Big]\Big|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\Big[\Big||\phi(x_{T})|+\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,\Theta)|ds\Big|^{\beta}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[|\phi(x_{T})-\phi(0)+\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,\Theta)-g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)+g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\Big|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\big|C|x_{T}|+\phi(0)\big|^{\beta}+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|C(|x|+|y|+|z^{1}+|z^{2}|+||\tilde{z}^{1}||+||\tilde{z}^{2}||)

\displaystyle+g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\Big|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[C^{\beta}|x_{T}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}C(|x|+|y|+|z^{1}|+|z^{2}|+||\tilde{z}^{1}||+||\tilde{z}^{2}||)ds\Big|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\Big|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[C^{\beta}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}C|x|ds\bigg|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}C|y|ds\Big|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\Big|\int_{0}^{T}C|z^{i}|ds\Big|^{\beta}+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}C||\tilde{z}^{i}||ds\Big|^{\beta}\bigg)+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\Big|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[(C^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+C^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\Big|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+C^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\Big|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\Big|^{\beta}\bigg].

We need the following lemma (
[
16
]
, Lemma 3.1).

Lemma 2.6
.

For any
\beta\geq 2
, we have

E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg],\ i=1,2.

(2.81)

Then, by Lemma
2.2
and B-D-G’s inequality, we can get the estimate

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.82)

\displaystyle\leq E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}z^{i}_{s}dW^{i}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i}_{(s,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds)\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigg(|y_{t}|^{\beta}+|y_{0}|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}g(s,\Theta(s))ds\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(s,\Theta(s))|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]+E\bigg[|\phi(x_{T})|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}g(s,\Theta(s))ds\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(s,\Theta)-g(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)+g(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle+E\bigg[C^{\beta}|x_{T}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}g(s,\Theta)-g(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)+g(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)ds\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[(1+C^{\beta}T^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+(C^{\beta}+C^{\beta}T^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+C^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg].

From (
2.80
), we have

\displaystyle(1-T^{\beta}C_{\beta})E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[(C^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+C^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg].

Choosing
T_{1}
small enough such that
T_{1}^{\beta}C_{\beta}<1
, and for every
0\leq T\leq T_{1}
, we get

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[(C^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+C^{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

(2.83)

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg].

From (
2.82
), we also have

\displaystyle(1-C_{\beta}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}})E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[(1+C^{\beta}T^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+(C^{\beta}+C^{\beta}T^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg].

Then combining with the estimate (
2.83
), we get

\displaystyle(1-C_{\beta,T}T^{\frac{\beta}{2}})E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[(C^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg].

Choosing
0\leq T_{2}\leq T_{1}
small enough such that
C_{\beta,T_{2}}T_{2}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}<1
, and for every
0\leq T\leq T_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.84)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[(C^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta})\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg],

where the constant
C_{\beta,T}
changed every step. Next, we begin to deal with the forward equation in (
2.10
). First, since

\displaystyle|x_{t}|^{\beta}

\displaystyle=C_{\beta}\bigg[|x_{0}|^{\beta}+\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}b(s,\Theta)ds\bigg|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{i}(s,\Theta)dW^{i}_{s}\bigg|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(s,x,y,e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds)\bigg|^{\beta}\bigg],

by B-D-G’s inequality and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,\Theta)|ds\bigg]^{\beta}

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,\Theta)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,x,y,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,\Theta)-b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)+b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg]^{\beta}

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,\Theta)-\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)+\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,x,y,e)-f_{i}(s,0,0,e)+f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E[|x_{0}|^{\beta}]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}C(|x|+|y|+|z^{1}|+|z^{2}|+||\tilde{z}^{1}||+||\tilde{z}^{2}||)ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]

(2.85)

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}K^{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})

\displaystyle+L^{2}_{\sigma_{i}}(|z^{1}|^{2}+|z^{2}|^{2}+||\tilde{z}^{1}||^{2}+||\tilde{z}^{2}||^{2})+|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{2}(e)(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[C^{\beta}\bigg(T^{\beta}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x|^{\beta}+T^{\beta}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y|^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}K^{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+L^{2}_{\sigma_{i}}(|z^{1}|^{2}+|z^{2}|^{2}+||\tilde{z}^{1}||^{2}+||\tilde{z}^{2}||^{2})ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{2}(e)(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[C^{\beta}\bigg(T^{\beta}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x|^{\beta}+T^{\beta}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y|^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}(|x|^{\beta}+|y|^{\beta})+(L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)(|x|^{\beta}+|y|^{\beta})\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg].

Here we have used the fact that, for
i=1,2
,

E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{2}(e)(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)(|x|^{\beta}+|y|^{\beta})\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg].

(2.86)

Indeed, we have

E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{2}(e)|x|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)|x|^{\beta}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg],\ i=1,2.

Set
H_{i,t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{2}(e)|x|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)
, and

\displaystyle H_{i,T}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\leq T}(H^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{i,s}-H^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{i,s-})=\sum_{s\leq T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\bigg(|H_{i,s-}+\rho^{2}(e)|x|^{2}|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}-H^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{i,s-}\bigg)N_{i}(de,\{s\})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\bigg(|H_{i,s-}+\rho^{2}(e)|x|^{2}|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}-H^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{i,s-}\bigg)N_{i}(de,ds)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\bigg(H^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{i,s-}+\rho^{\beta}(e)|x|^{\beta}\bigg)N_{i}(de,ds),

then

\displaystyle EH_{i,T}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\bigg(H^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{i,s-}+\rho^{\beta}(e)|x|^{\beta}\bigg)\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\int_{0}^{T}H^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{i,s}ds+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)|x|^{\beta}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg].

From Gronwall’s inequality, we get

E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{2}(e)|x|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)|x|^{\beta}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg].

(2.87)

Then we can manage (
2.85
) as follows

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]+\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\Big]

\displaystyle\times\bigg(E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x|^{\beta}\bigg]+E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y|^{\beta}\bigg]\bigg)

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})E\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg].

From (
2.83
) and (
2.84
), we then have

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]+\bigg\{\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)\Big]\Big[1+C_{\beta,T}(C^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta})\Big]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})C_{\beta,T}(C^{\beta}+T^{\beta}C^{\beta})\bigg\}E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x|^{\beta}\bigg]+\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})

\displaystyle+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})\Big]C_{\beta,T}E|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})

\displaystyle+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})\Big]C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]+C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},T}E\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x|^{\beta}\bigg]+\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})

\displaystyle+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})\Big]C_{\beta,T}E|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})

\displaystyle+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})\Big]C_{\beta,T}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg].

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(1-C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},T})E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]\leq C_{\beta}E\big[|x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]

\displaystyle+\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})\Big]C_{\beta,T}E|\phi(0)|^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\Big[C_{\beta}(T^{\beta}C^{\beta}+T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}K^{\beta})+C_{\beta,T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\rho^{\beta}(e)\nu_{i}(de)+C_{\beta}(T^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C^{\beta}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{1}}+L^{\beta}_{\sigma_{2}})\Big]C_{\beta,T}

\displaystyle\times E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg]+C_{\beta}E\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg].

Choosing
0\leq T_{3}\leq T_{2}
and
L_{\sigma}
small enough such that
C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},T_{3}}<1
, and for every
0\leq T\leq T_{3}
, we get

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},T}E\bigg[|x_{0}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}

(2.88)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg].

Finally, from (
2.83
), (
2.84
) and (
2.88
), we can choose
\bar{T}=\min\{T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}\}
, and for every
0\leq T\leq\bar{T}
such that

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}E\bigg[|x_{0}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|ds\bigg)^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}ds\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,ds)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg],

(2.89)

where the positive constant
C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}
depends on
\beta
and Lipschitz constants
K,\rho,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}}
. The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 2.6
.

Similar to Theorem
2.6
, under
(A4), (A5)
, we can also obtain the
L^{\beta}(\beta\geq 2)
-estimate for fully-coupled FBSDEP (
2.10
) in
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\beta}[0,T]
, we immediately give the similar result which is more general than Theorem
2.6
as follows.

Theorem 2.7
.

Suppose that
(A4), (A5)
hold. Then, for any
\beta\geq 2
, suppose that
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{2},\tilde{z}^{2})
is the solution to (
2.10
), there exists a sufficiently small constant
\tilde{\tilde{T}}>0
depending on
K,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},L_{f_{1}},\\
L_{f_{2}},\rho
and some constant
C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2}}
depending on
\beta
and Lipschitz constants
K,\rho,L_{\sigma_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}},L_{f_{1}},\\
L_{f_{2}}
such that, for every
0\leq T\leq\tilde{\tilde{T}}

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|x_{t}|^{\beta}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|y_{t}|^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|z^{i}|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\tilde{z}^{i}|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]

(2.90)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,K,\rho,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2}}E\bigg[|x_{0}|^{\beta}+|\phi(0)|^{\beta}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|g(t,0,0,0,0,0,0)|dt\bigg)^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|b(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|dt\bigg)^{\beta}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,0,0)|^{2}dt\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|f_{i}(s,0,0,0,0,e)|^{2}N_{i}(de,dt)\bigg)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\bigg].

In fact, due to Lemma
2.6
, Theorem
2.7
deduces to Theorem
2.6
when changing
(A5)
into
(A6)
.

Remark 2.7
.

Theorem
2.3
and Theorem
2.7
are better and more general results compared to Theorem
2.2
and Theorem
2.6
, when extending the stronger Assumption
(A6)
into a weaker one
(A5)
. Moreover, they are nice results for studying the stochastic control problem of fully coupled FBSDEPs in the future. However, in our setting of this paper, the partially observed stochastic control problem involves a special fully coupled FBSDEP, and it is enough to solve them by Theorem
2.2
and Theorem
2.6
.

Therefore, we still use the space
\mathcal{M}^{\beta}[0,T]
in the following discussion.

2.2
Decoupling random field method for solvability of FBSDEPs

In the following, under
(A1)
, for any
u\in\mathcal{U}_{ad}[0,T]
, we show that the state equation (
2.9
) admits a unique solution
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
for any given time
T
under
P
.

Observing that (
2.9
) is a kind of special fully coupled FBSDEP in which
\sigma_{i},f_{i}
do not contain
(z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
, then we can give the following theorem which is similar to the main result of
[
32
]

Theorem 2.8
.

Let
{\bf(A4)}
hold or
{\bf(A1)}
hold without control
u
, then there exists an
L^{2}
-solution
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
to FBSDEP:

\left\{\begin{aligned} dx_{t}&=\tilde{b}_{1}\big(t,\Theta(t)\big)dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sigma_{i}(t,x_{t})dW^{i}_{t}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(t,x_{t-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dt),\\
-dy_{t}&=g\big(t,\Theta(t)\big)dt-\sum_{i=1}^{2}z^{i}_{t}dW^{i}_{t}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i}_{(t,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dt),\quad t\in[0,T],\\
x_{0}&=x_{0},\ \ y_{T}=\phi(x_{T}),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.91)

such that
x
satisfies the following equation

\displaystyle x_{t}

\displaystyle=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}_{1}\Big(s,x_{s},\theta(s,x_{s}),\partial_{x}\theta(s,x_{s})\sigma_{1}(s,x_{s}),\partial_{x}\theta(s,x_{s})\sigma_{2}(s,x_{s}),

(2.92)

\displaystyle\theta(s,x_{s}+f_{1}(s,x_{s},e))-\theta(s,x_{s}),\theta(s,x_{s}+f_{2}(s,x_{s},e))-\theta(s,x_{s})\Big)ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{i}(s,x_{s})dW^{i}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(s,x_{s-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds),

where, under some suitable conditions (see
[
32
]
),
\theta(t,x)
is the unique
C^{1,2}_{b}([0,T],\mathbb{R})
-solution with bounded
\partial_{x}\theta
and
\partial_{xx}\theta
to the following partial integro-differential equation (PIDE for short):

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{x}\theta\Big\{\tilde{b}_{1}\big(t,x,\theta,\partial_{x}\theta\sigma_{1}(t,x),\partial_{x}\theta\sigma_{2}(t,x),\theta(t,x+f_{1}(t,x,e))-\theta,\theta(t,x+f_{2}(t,x,e))-\theta\big)\\
&-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(t,x,e)\nu_{i}(de)\Big\}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\partial^{2}_{xx}\theta(\sigma_{i}(t,x))^{2}\\
&+g\big(t,x,\theta,\partial_{x}\theta\sigma_{1}(t,x),\partial_{x}\theta\sigma_{2}(t,x),\theta(t,x+f_{1}(t,x,e))-\theta,\theta(t,x+f_{2}(t,x,e))-\theta\big)\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}(\theta(t,x+f_{i}(t,x,e))-\theta)\nu_{i}(de)+\partial_{t}\theta=0,\qquad\theta(T,x)=\phi(x),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.93)

where
\theta,\partial_{t}\theta,\partial_{x}\theta,\partial^{2}_{xx}\theta
are everywhere evaluated at
(t,x)
(here, we omit the arguments
(t,x)
for simplicity). Moreover,
y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2}
are explicitly expressed via the solution
\theta
to PIDE (
2.93
) by the formulas

\displaystyle y_{t}=\theta(t,x_{t}),\ z^{i}_{t}=\partial_{x}\theta(t,x_{t})\sigma_{i}(t,x_{t}),\ \tilde{z}^{i}_{t}=\theta(t,x_{t-}+f_{i}(t,x_{t-},e))-\theta(t,x_{t-}),\ i=1,2,

(2.94)

and the
L^{2}
-solution
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
is unique in
\mathcal{M}^{2}[0,T]
.

Proof.

First, defining
\tilde{\tilde{b}}_{1}(t,x)=\tilde{b}_{1}\big(t,x,\theta(t,x),\partial_{x}\theta(t,x)\sigma_{1}(t,x),\partial_{x}\theta(t,x)\sigma_{2}(t,x),\theta(t,x+f_{1}(t,x,e))\\
-\theta(t,x),\theta(t,x+f_{2}(t,x,e))-\theta(t,x)\big)
, the assumption
(A4)
(or
(A1)
) and boundedness of
\partial_{x}\theta,\partial_{xx}\theta
can guarantee that
\tilde{\tilde{b}}_{1},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2}
satisfy the Lipschitz condition w.r.t
x
and

E\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\tilde{\tilde{b}}_{1}(t,0)\Big|^{2}dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}E\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{i}(t,0)|^{2}dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}E\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\int_{0}^{T}|f_{i}(t,0,e)|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)dt<\infty.

(2.95)

Then (
2.92
) admits a unique solution
x
. Next, by applying time-dependent Itô’s formula (
Lemma 6
,
[
32
]
) to
\theta(t,x)
and combining (
2.93
) satisfied by
\theta(t,x)
, we can prove the existence of solution
y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2}
defined by (
2.94
) of BSDEP in (
2.91
). Finally, the proof of uniqueness is similar to that of
[
32
]
, so we omit the details.
∎

The idea above mainly motivates us to consider the decoupling function
\theta
, so we study the following FBSDEP with random coefficients and initial value
x_{0}=\xi
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} x^{0,\xi}_{t}&=\xi+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}_{1}\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi}(r)\big)dr+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi}_{r})dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\\
y^{0,\xi}_{t}&=\phi(\omega,x_{T}^{0,\xi})+\int_{t}^{T}g\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi}(r)\big)dr-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}z^{i,0,\xi}_{r}dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(r,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\quad t\in[0,T],\end{aligned}\right.

(2.96)

where coefficients
\tilde{b}_{1},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},g
and
\phi
are random, and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2}
are independent of

(y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
.

Let
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,
\eta
be an
\mathcal{F}_{t_{1}}
-measurable square integrable random variable, and
\varphi(\omega,x)
be a random field such that
\varphi
is
\mathcal{F}_{t_{2}}
-measurable for any fixed
x
and uniformly Lipschitz continuous in
x
with a Lipschitz constant
\bar{K}
. Consider the following FBSDEP over
[t_{1},t_{2}]
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} x^{0,\xi}_{t}&=\eta+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\tilde{b}_{1}\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi}(r)\big)dr+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\sigma_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi}_{r})dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\\
y^{0,\xi}_{t}&=\varphi(\omega,x_{t_{2}}^{0,\xi})+\int_{t}^{t_{2}}g\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi}(r)\big)dr-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{t_{2}}z^{i,0,\xi}_{r}dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi}_{(r,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\quad t\in[t_{1},t_{2}].\end{aligned}\right.

(2.97)

Note that, by applying Theorem
2.1
, there exists a constant
\tilde{T}(\bar{K})
depending on the Lipschitz constants in
(A4)
and Lipschitz constant
\bar{K}
of
\varphi
such that whenever
t_{2}-t_{1}\leq\tilde{T}(\bar{K})
, FBSDEP (
2.97
) admits a unique
L^{2}
-solution.

Now, we give the following result, whose non-jump case is introduced by Cvitanić and Zhang
[
4
]
(see also
Lemma 4.1

[
21
]
).

Theorem 2.9
.

Let
(A4)
hold, and there exists a random field
\theta(t,\omega,x)
such that

(i)
\theta(T,\omega,x)=\phi(\omega,x)
;

(ii) For each
(t,x)
,
\theta
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable;

(iii)
|\theta(t,x_{1})-\theta(t,x_{2})|\leq\bar{K}|x_{1}-x_{2}|
,
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
;

(iv) For any
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
such that
|t_{2}-t_{1}|\leq\tilde{T}(\bar{K})
, where
\tilde{T}(\bar{K})
, depending on the Lipschitz constants in
(A4)
and Lipschitz constant
\bar{K}
, is a sufficient small constant, the unique solution to FBSDEP (
2.97
) over
[t_{1},t_{2}]
with terminal condition
\varphi(\omega,x):=\theta(t_{2},\omega,x)
satisfies
y^{0,\xi}_{t_{1}}=\theta(t_{1},\omega,x^{0,\xi}_{t_{1}})
.

Then, for any given
T>0
, FBSDEP (
2.96
) admits a unique
L^{2}
-solution on
[0,T]
and
y^{0,\xi}_{t}=\theta(t,\omega,x_{t}^{0,\xi})
.

Proof.

Existence.
Let
0=t_{0}<\cdots<t_{n}=T
be a partition of
[0,T]
such that
t_{k}-t_{k-1}\leq\tilde{T}(\bar{K})
for
k=1,\dots,n
. Denote
x_{0}^{0,\xi,0}:=\xi
. For
k=1,\dots,n
, let
(x^{k},y^{k},z^{1,k},z^{2,k},\tilde{z}^{1,k},\tilde{z}^{2,k})
be the unique solution to the following FBSDEP over
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} x^{0,\xi,k}_{s}&=x^{0,\xi,k-1}_{t_{k-1}}+\int_{t_{k-1}}^{s}\tilde{b}_{1}\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi,k}(r)\big)dr+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{s}\sigma_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k}_{r})dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{s}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\\
y^{0,\xi,k}_{s}&=\theta(t_{k},\omega,x_{t_{k}}^{0,\xi,k})+\int_{s}^{t_{k}}g\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi,k}(r)\big)dr-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{k}}z^{i,0,\xi,k}_{r}dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i,0,\xi,k}_{(r,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.98)

Define

\displaystyle x_{t}^{0,\xi}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}x_{t}^{0,\xi,k}1_{[t_{k-1},t_{k})}(t)+x_{T}^{0,\xi,n}1_{\{T\}}(t),

(2.99)

\displaystyle y_{t}^{0,\xi}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}y_{t}^{0,\xi,k}1_{[t_{k-1},t_{k})}(t)+y_{T}^{0,\xi,n}1_{\{T\}}(t),

\displaystyle z_{t}^{i,0,\xi}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}z_{t}^{i,0,\xi,k}1_{[t_{k-1},t_{k})}(t)+z_{T}^{i,0,\xi,n}1_{\{T\}}(t),\ i=1,2,

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{(t,e)}^{i,0,\xi}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\tilde{z}_{(t,e)}^{i,0,\xi,k}1_{[t_{k-1},t_{k})}(t)+\tilde{z}_{(T,e)}^{i,0,\xi,n}1_{\{T\}}(t),\ i=1,2.

Note that we should consider a necessary situation that the jumps occur at the time
t_{k},k=1,\dots,n
. Without loss of generality, we consider that the jump appears at
t_{k}
and for forward equation,
x_{t}^{0,\xi,k}
is an adapted RCLL process on
[t_{k-1},t_{k}],k=1,\dots,n
. Due to the appearance of jump at
t_{k}
, it can be understood by the following equation from (
2.98
):

\displaystyle(i)\ x^{0,\xi,k+1}_{t_{k}}

\displaystyle=x^{0,\xi,k}_{t_{k}}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k}}\tilde{b}_{1}\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi,k+1}(r)\big)dr+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k}}\sigma_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k+1}_{r})dW^{i}_{r}

(2.100)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k+1}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr)

\displaystyle=x^{0,\xi,k}_{t_{k}},

\displaystyle(ii)\ x^{0,\xi,k}_{t_{k}}

\displaystyle=x^{0,\xi,k-1}_{t_{k-1}}+\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\tilde{b}_{1}\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{0,\xi,k}(r)\big)dr+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\sigma_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k}_{r})dW^{i}_{r}

(2.101)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr).

In (i),
x_{t_{k}}^{0,\xi,k+1}
can be regarded as the initial value of
x_{t}^{0,\xi,k+1}
on
[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
. In (ii),
x_{t_{k}}^{0,\xi,k}
can be regraded as the terminal value of
x_{t}^{0,\xi,k}
on
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
.

Because there exists a jump at
t_{k}
, the integrals, especially for
\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k+1}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,\\
dr)
in (i), are equal to 0. However, the integrals, like
\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{0,\xi,k}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr)
in (ii), are not equal to 0.

In particular, when
f_{i}\equiv 1
,
\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}1\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr)=\tilde{N}_{i}(\mathcal{E}_{i},t_{k})=0
, i.e., the quantity of jump at
t_{k}
which is the initial time of
[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
, is 0 for (i). And
\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}1\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr)=\tilde{N}_{i}(\mathcal{E}_{i},[t_{k-1},t_{k}])=1
, i.e., the quantity of jump on time interval
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
is 1 for (ii), and the jump just occur at
t_{k}
which is the terminal time of
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
. We have similar analysis for
y_{t}^{0,\xi,k}
of the backward equation.

Based on the above analysis, we have
x_{t_{k-1}}^{0,\xi,k}=x_{t_{k-1}}^{0,\xi,k-1}
and
y_{t_{k-1}}^{0,\xi,k}=\theta(t_{k-1},\omega,x^{0,\xi,k}_{t_{k-1}})=\theta(t_{k-1},\omega,x^{0,\xi,k-1}_{t_{k-1}})=y_{t_{k-1}}^{0,\xi,k-1}
.

Therefore, for any two adjacent intervals,
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
and
[t_{k},t_{k+1}],k=1,\dots,n-1
, the initial value of
(x^{0,\xi,k+1},y^{0,\xi,k+1})
at initial
t_{k}
of current interval
[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
is equal to the terminal value of
(x^{0,\xi,k},y^{0,\xi,k})
at terminal
t_{k}
of previous interval
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
. Moreover, we can connect these small pieces naturally and can check straightforwardly that
(x^{0,\xi},y^{0,\xi},z^{1,0,\xi},z^{2,0,\xi},\tilde{z}^{1,0,\xi},\tilde{z}^{2,0,\xi})
solves (
2.96
).

Uniqueness.
The proof is similar to that in
[
4
]
.

Finally, for any
t\in[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
, consider the FBSDEP on
[t,t_{k}]
, we see that
y^{0,\xi}_{t}=\theta(t,\omega,x_{t}^{0,\xi})
. The proof is complete.
∎

Now, we give a sufficient condition for the existence of the random field
\theta
in Theorem
2.9
.

Theorem 2.10
.

Let
(A4)
hold.
\tilde{b}_{1},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2}
are continuously differentiable in
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\\
\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
and assume that

\tilde{b}_{1y}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{b}_{1z^{i}}\sigma_{ix}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}\nu_{i}(de)=0.

(2.102)

Then there exists a random field
\theta
satisfying the conditions in Theorem
2.9
, and consequently FBSDEP (
2.96
) admits a unique solution.

Proof.

Step 1.
Let
K
denote the Lipschitz constant of
\tilde{b}_{1},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},g
with respect to
(x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})
and
K_{0}
the Lipschitz constant of
\phi
with respect to
x
. Denote

\bar{K}:=e^{\hat{K}T}(1+K_{0})-1,

(2.103)

where
\hat{K}:=(2+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\nu_{i}(\mathcal{E}_{i}))K^{2}+2K
and let
\tilde{T}(\bar{K})
be the constant introduced in Theorem
2.9
. Fix a partition
0=t_{0}<\cdots<t_{n}=T
such that
\Delta t_{k}=t_{k}-t_{k-1}\leq\tilde{T}(\bar{K}),k=1,\dots,n
. For each
(t,x)\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]\times\mathbb{R}
, consider the following FBSDEP on
[t,t_{n}]
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} x^{t,x}_{s}&=x+\int_{t}^{s}\tilde{b}_{1}\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{t,x}(r)\big)dr+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{s}\sigma_{i}(r,\omega,x^{t,x}_{r})dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{s}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{i}(r,\omega,x^{t,x}_{r-},e)\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\\
y^{t,x}_{s}&=\theta(t_{n},\omega,x_{t_{n}}^{t,x})+\int_{s}^{t_{n}}g\big(r,\omega,\Theta^{t,x}(r)\big)dr-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{n}}z^{i,t,x}_{r}dW^{i}_{r}\\
&\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\tilde{z}^{i,t,x}_{(r,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.104)

By Theorem
2.1
, FBSDEP (
2.104
) admits a unique solution. Define
\theta(t,x):=y^{t,x}_{t},t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
.

Step 2.
Given
x_{1},x_{2}
and
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
, denote
\Delta x:=x_{1}-x_{2}
,
\Delta\Theta:=\Theta^{t,x_{1}}-\Theta^{t,x_{2}}
. Then
\Delta\Theta
satisfies the following FBSDEP:

\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta x_{s}&=\Delta x+\int_{t}^{s}[\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1x}(r)\Delta x_{r}+\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1y}(r)\Delta y_{r}+\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1z^{1}}(r)\Delta z^{1}_{r}+\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1z^{2}}(r)\Delta z^{2}_{r}\\
&\quad+\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1\tilde{z}^{1}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\Delta\tilde{z}^{1}_{(r,e)}\nu_{1}(de)+\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1\tilde{z}^{2}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\Delta\tilde{z}^{2}_{(r,e)}\nu_{2}(de)]dr\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{s}\sigma^{\prime}_{ix}(r)\Delta x_{r}dW^{i}_{r}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{s}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f^{\prime}_{ix}(r,e)\Delta x_{r-}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr)\\
\Delta y_{s}&=\phi^{\prime}_{x}(t_{n})\Delta x_{t_{n}}+\int_{s}^{t_{n}}[g^{\prime}_{x}(r)\Delta x_{r}+g^{\prime}_{y}(r)\Delta y_{r}+g^{\prime}_{z^{1}}(r)\Delta z^{1}_{r}+g^{\prime}_{z^{2}}(r)\Delta z^{2}_{r}\\
&\quad+g^{\prime}_{\tilde{z}^{1}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\Delta\tilde{z}^{1}_{(r,e)}\nu_{1}(de)+g^{\prime}_{\tilde{z}^{2}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\Delta\tilde{z}^{2}_{(r,e)}\nu_{2}(de)]dr\\
&\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{n}}\Delta z^{i}_{r}dW^{i}_{r}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\Delta\tilde{z}^{i}_{(r,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.105)

where
\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1x}(r):=\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{b}_{1x}(r,\omega,\Theta^{t,x_{2}}(r)+\lambda(\Theta^{t,x_{1}}(r)-\Theta^{t,x_{2}}(r)))d\lambda
and
\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1y}(r),\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1z^{i}}(r),\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}(r),\sigma^{\prime}_{ix}(r),\\
f^{\prime}_{ix}(r,e),g^{\prime}_{x}(r),g^{\prime}_{y}(r),g^{\prime}_{z^{i}}(r),g^{\prime}_{\tilde{z}^{i}}(r),\phi^{\prime}_{x}(t_{n}),i=1,2,
have similar definition. Moreover,
|\alpha_{\beta}(r,e)|\leq K
, for
\alpha=\tilde{b}_{1},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2}
and
g
,
\beta=x,y,z^{1},z^{2},\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2}
, and
|\phi_{x}^{\prime}(t_{n})|\leq K_{0}
. Then, FBSDEP (
2.105
) also satisfies
(A4)
. Here, we note that FBSDEP (
2.105
) is on time interval
[t,t_{n}]
, and all the arguments in Theorem
2.1
and Theorem
2.6
hold by replacing the expectation with conditional expectation. By Theorem
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sup_{t\leq s\leq t_{n}}(|\Delta x_{s}|^{2}+|\Delta y_{s}|^{2})+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\int_{t}^{t_{n}}|\Delta z^{i}_{s}|^{2}ds+\int_{t}^{t_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|\Delta\tilde{z}^{i}_{(s,e)}|^{2}\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\bigg]\leq C|\Delta x|^{2},

(2.106)

where the precise form of constant
C
will be considered later. In particular, this implies

|\theta(t,x_{1})-\theta(t,x_{2})|^{2}=|\Delta y_{t}|^{2}\leq E[|\Delta y_{t}|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq E[\sup_{t\leq s\leq t_{n}}|\Delta y_{s}|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq C|\Delta x|^{2}

(2.107)

i.e.,

|\theta(t,x_{1})-\theta(t,x_{2})|\leq\sqrt{C}|x_{1}-x_{2}|.

(2.108)

That is, for
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
,
\theta(t,x)
is uniformly Lipschitz continuous in
x
.

Step 3.
Let
\eta=\sum_{j=1}^{m}x_{j}1_{E_{j}}
, where
x_{1},\cdots,x_{m}\in\mathbb{R}
and
E_{1},\cdots,E_{m}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
form a partition of
\Omega
. One can check straightforwardly that
\Theta^{t,\eta}:=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Theta^{t,x_{j}}1_{E_{j}}
satisfies FBSDEP (
2.104
) with initial value
x^{t,\eta}_{t}:=\eta
. In particular, this implies that

y^{t,\eta}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}y^{t,x_{j}}_{t}1_{E_{j}}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\theta(t,\omega,x_{j})1_{E_{j}}=\theta(t,\omega,\sum_{j=1}^{m}x_{j}1_{E_{j}})=\theta(t,\omega,\eta).

(2.109)

Moreover, for general
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable square integrable
\eta
, there exist
\{\eta_{m},m\geq 1\}
taking the above form such that
\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E[|\eta_{m}-\eta|^{2}]=0
. Denote again by
\Theta^{t,\eta}
the unique solution to FBSDEP (
2.104
) with initial value
x^{t,\eta}_{t}=\eta
. By the previous analysis, we obtain that

E[|y^{t,\eta}-y^{t,\eta_{m}}|^{2}]\leq CE[|\eta-\eta_{m}|^{2}]\rightarrow 0.

(2.110)

Since
\theta(t,x)
is Lipschitz continuous in
x
, we get

y_{t}^{t,\eta}=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}y_{t}^{t,\eta_{m}}=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\theta(t,\omega,\eta_{m})=\theta(t,\omega,\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\eta_{m})=\theta(t,\omega,\eta).

(2.111)

Step 4.
We now get a more precise estimate for constant
C
in (
2.108
). Since
\tilde{b}_{1},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},f_{1},f_{2},g
and
\phi
are continuously differentiable, by standard arguments, one can see that
\theta(t,x)
is differentiable in
x
with
\theta_{x}(t,x)=\nabla y^{t,x}_{t}
where

\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla x_{s}&=1+\int_{t}^{s}[\tilde{b}_{1x}(r)\nabla x_{r}+\tilde{b}_{1y}(r)\nabla y_{r}+\tilde{b}_{1z^{1}}(r)\nabla z^{1}_{r}+\tilde{b}_{1z^{2}}(r)\nabla z^{2}_{r}\\
&\quad+\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{1}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\nabla\tilde{z}^{1}_{(r,e)}\nu_{1}(de)+\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{2}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\nabla\tilde{z}^{2}_{(r,e)}\nu_{2}(de)]dr\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{s}\sigma_{ix}(r)\nabla x_{r}dW^{i}_{r}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{s}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}(r,e)\nabla x_{r-}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr)\\
\nabla y_{s}&=\phi_{x}(\omega,x_{t_{n}})\nabla x_{t_{n}}+\int_{s}^{t_{n}}[g_{x}(r)\nabla x_{r}+g_{y}(r)\nabla y_{r}+g_{z^{1}}(r)\nabla z^{1}_{r}+g_{z^{2}}(r)\nabla z^{2}_{r}\\
&\quad+g_{\tilde{z}^{1}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\nabla\tilde{z}^{1}_{(r,e)}\nu_{1}(de)+g_{\tilde{z}^{2}}(r)\int_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\nabla\tilde{z}^{2}_{(r,e)}\nu_{2}(de)]dr\\
&\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{n}}\nabla z^{i}_{r}dW^{i}_{r}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{s}^{t_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\nabla\tilde{z}^{i}_{(r,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,dr),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.112)

where
\tilde{b}_{1x}(r):=\tilde{b}_{1x}(r,\omega,\Theta(r))
and other terms are defined similarly.

Denote

\displaystyle\hat{y}_{s}:

\displaystyle=\nabla y_{s}(\nabla x_{s})^{-1},\ \bar{z}^{i}_{s}:=\nabla z^{i}_{s}(\nabla x_{s})^{-1},\ \bar{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}:=\nabla\tilde{z}^{i}_{(s,e)}(\nabla x_{s})^{-1},

(2.113)

\displaystyle\hat{z}^{i}_{s}:

\displaystyle=\bar{z}^{i}_{s}-\sigma_{ix}\hat{y}_{s},\ \hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}:=\bar{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}(1+f_{ix})^{-1}-\hat{y}_{s}f_{ix}(1+f_{ix})^{-1},\ i=1,2.

Applying Itô’s formula, we obtain

\displaystyle d(\nabla x_{s})^{-1}

\displaystyle=-(\nabla x_{s})^{-1}\bigg[\tilde{b}_{1x}+\tilde{b}_{1y}\hat{y}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\tilde{b}_{1z^{i}}\bar{z}^{i}_{s}+\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\bar{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\nu_{i}(de)-\sigma^{2}_{ix}

(2.114)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}^{2}(1+f_{ix})^{-1}\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)\bigg]ds-(\nabla x_{s})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sigma_{ix}dW^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle-(\nabla x_{s})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}(1+f_{ix})^{-1}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds),

\displaystyle d\hat{y}_{s}

\displaystyle=-\hat{y}_{s}\bigg[\tilde{b}_{1x}+\tilde{b}_{1y}\hat{y}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg(\tilde{b}_{1z^{i}}\bar{z}^{i}_{s}+\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\bar{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\nu_{i}(de)-\sigma_{ix}^{2}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f^{2}_{ix}(1+f_{ix})^{-1}\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)\bigg]ds-\bigg[g_{x}+g_{y}\hat{y}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}g_{z^{i}}\bar{z}^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}g_{\tilde{z}^{i}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\bar{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\nu_{i}(de)\bigg]ds-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}(1+f_{ix})^{-1}\bar{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\nu_{i}(de)ds

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sigma_{ix}\bar{z}^{i}_{s}ds+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\hat{z}^{i}_{s}dW^{i}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds).

Note that
\bar{z}^{i}_{s}=\hat{z}^{i}_{s}+\sigma_{ix}\hat{y}_{s}
,
\bar{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}=\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}(1+f_{ix})+\hat{y}_{s}f_{ix}
,
i=1,2
, we obtain

\displaystyle d\hat{y}_{s}

\displaystyle=-\bigg[g_{x}+\bigg(\tilde{b}_{1x}+g_{y}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}g_{z^{i}}\sigma_{ix}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}g_{\tilde{z}^{i}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)\hat{y}_{s}

(2.115)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\tilde{b}_{1y}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{b}_{1z^{i}}\sigma_{ix}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}\nu_{i}(de)\bigg)\hat{y}^{2}_{s}\bigg]ds

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\big[\tilde{b}_{1z^{i}}\hat{y}_{s}+g_{z^{i}}+\sigma_{ix}\big]\hat{z}^{i}_{s}ds+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\hat{z}^{i}_{s}dW^{i}_{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\tilde{N}_{i}(de,ds)

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigg[(\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}\hat{y}_{s}+g_{\tilde{z}^{i}})\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}(1+f_{ix})\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\nu_{i}(de)+\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}f_{ix}\hat{\tilde{z}}^{i}_{(s,e)}\nu_{i}(de)\bigg]ds.

We note that
\hat{y}_{s}=\theta_{x}(s,x_{s})
and is bounded for
s\in[t,t_{n}]\subset[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
. Denote

\displaystyle d\hat{P}:

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}(\tilde{b}_{1z^{i}}\hat{y}_{s}+g_{z^{i}}+\sigma_{ix})dW^{i}_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}|\tilde{b}_{1z^{i}}\hat{y}_{s}+g_{z^{i}}+\sigma_{ix}|^{2}ds

(2.116)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}\log[(\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}\hat{y}_{s}+g_{\tilde{z}^{i}}+1)(1+f_{ix})]N_{i}(de,ds)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}[1-(\tilde{b}_{1\tilde{z}^{i}}\hat{y}_{s}+g_{\tilde{z}^{i}}+1)(1+f_{ix})]\nu_{i}(de)ds\bigg\}dP,

and introduce

\displaystyle\Gamma_{s}
